I'd say this sort of low baseline has been going on since... well, shortly after we moved into the older house. Do you have trouble falling asleep, staying asleep through the night, or waking up too early in the morning?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't really have trouble falling asleep. I'm usually so tired by the end of the day that I drop off pretty quick. But staying asleep... I don't know, I guess I wake up a few times during the night. I don't really look at the clock, so I'm not sure if it's too early or what, but I just don't feel rested when the alarm finally goes off.\n\nDoctor: When you wake up during those nighttime awakenings, do you find it hard to fall back asleep, or do you drift back off pretty easily?\n\nPatient: I usually drift back off pretty easily. I might just adjust my pillow or shift around a bit, and then I'm back asleep. It's not like I'm lying there awake for hours or anything. I just... I don't know, I just wake up a few times, and then go right back to sleep.\n\nDoctor: Besides the low mood and fatigue you've described, have you been experiencing persistent worry or anxiety? If so, what kinds of things do you tend to worry about?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, I worry about normal day-to-day things sometimes, but... no, I wouldn't say I have persistent anxiety. So, no, no real changes there.\n\nDoctor: Over these past two years since you moved into the house, has there been any stretch of time — even a week or two — where you felt like your normal self again, or has this low mood and fatigue been pretty much constant throughout that entire period?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I haven't really noticed any stretch where I felt completely back to normal. It's been pretty much constant... just this steady, flat feeling and the tiredness the whole time. If there was a good week or two in there, I honestly don't remember it. It just feels like it's been this way since we moved.\n\nDoctor: Besides the fatigue and sleep issues you've mentioned, have you noticed any physical symptoms — like muscle aches, headaches, or a general sense of heaviness or tension in your body — that have been going on along with the tiredness?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I mean, I might get a normal headache every once in a while, but nothing out of the ordinary. My body doesn't really ache or feel tense or anything. It's honestly just... just the tiredness we talked about. So, no, no real physical pain or heaviness to go along with it.\n\nDoctor: Over these two years, have you ever had a period where you felt like you had more energy than usual, needed less sleep than normal, or felt like you were accomplishing things at a faster pace than your baseline?\n\nPatient: No, definitely not. I haven't had any bursts of energy or anything like that. I always need my sleep... probably more than I should, honestly. I haven't felt like I'm getting things done faster or better. It's just been that same low energy the whole time.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned the low mood and fatigue have been pretty constant for two years. But I want to be more precise: have there been any individual days, or even just a few days in a row, where you felt noticeably better — where the tiredness lifted or your mood felt lighter — before it came back again?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, I guess there might be days that aren't quite as bad as others, but... no, I wouldn't say the tiredness ever actually lifts. It doesn't really go away. It's just kind of always there in the background, so I can't really point to a specific day where I felt noticeably better or lighter.\n\nDoctor: During these past two years, has there been any stretch of time — say, two months or longer — where you felt completely free of this low mood and tiredness, even if it came back later?\n\nPatient: No, there hasn't been anything like that. I definitely haven't had a two-month break from it. Like I said, it's just been pretty constant for the last two years. I haven't had any stretch of time where I felt completely free of it.\n\nDoctor: I know you said the low mood and tiredness have been pretty constant, but I want to clarify: on your better days, would you say the tiredness gets noticeably lighter, or does it stay about the same level most days?\n\nPatient: It pretty much stays at the same level. Even if a day isn't quite as bad, the tiredness is still right there. It doesn't really get lighter. I think... I might just be dealing with it a little better on those days, but the actual tired feeling is always about the same.\n\nDoctor: Before you moved into the house two years ago, how was your mood and energy level? Would you say you felt your normal self back then, or had you already been experiencing this low mood and tiredness before the move?\n\nPatient: Before the move, I felt pretty normal. My energy and mood were fine back then. This... this low baseline I've been dealing with really just started shortly after we moved into the older house, which was a little over two years ago.\n\nDoctor: Just to be absolutely clear: from the time you moved into the house until right now, has there been even a single day where you felt completely free of both the low mood and the tiredness — where both symptoms were completely absent for that day?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, there hasn't been a single day where I woke up and just felt totally fine, with normal energy and a good mood. It's always there, even on the days that are a little easier to get through.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. My energy and mood were fine back then. I mean, there hasn't been a single day where I woke up and just felt totally fine, with normal energy and a good mood. It's always there, even on the days that are a little easier to get through.\n\nDoctor: When you think back over these two years, have there been any stretches of time — even just two or three weeks — where your mood felt noticeably better and you had more energy than usual, before it went back to this low baseline you've been describing?\n\nPatient: No, there haven't been any stretches like that. It's just been this same low feeling the whole time. I haven't had any periods where I felt noticeably better or had more energy.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. And by the time I've handled work and tried to deal with the house clutter, I just have very little energy left for anything else. I'm just constantly exhausted.\n\nDoctor: Besides feeling exhausted, how has your mood been? Would you say you've been feeling down or depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I suppose I have been. It's... well, most days there's just this low, gray mood running in the background. I mean, I can still go into the lab and talk to people just fine, but I just don't really feel light or excited about things anymore. Even stuff I used to really enjoy, like baking or taking out my telescope... I just don't have that same feeling about them the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way — this low mood and exhaustion? Has it been going on for weeks, months, or longer?\n\nPatient: It's been longer than a few months. I'd say this sort of low baseline has been going on since... well, shortly after we moved into the older house. That was a little over two years ago now.\n\nDoctor: When you say you wake up tired even after a full night's sleep, can you tell me more about your sleep itself? Do you have trouble falling asleep, staying asleep through the night, or waking up too early in the morning?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't really have trouble falling asleep. I'm usually so tired by the end of the day that I drop off pretty quick. But staying asleep... I don't know, I guess I wake up a few times during the night. I don't really look at the clock, so I'm not sure if it's too early or what, but I just don't feel rested when the alarm finally goes off.\n\nDoctor: When you wake up during those nighttime awakenings, do you find it hard to fall back asleep, or do you drift back off pretty easily?\n\nPatient: I usually drift back off pretty easily. I might just adjust my pillow or shift around a bit, and then I'm back asleep. It's not like I'm lying there awake for hours or anything. I just... I don't know, I just wake up a few times, and then go right back to sleep.\n\nDoctor: Besides the low mood and fatigue you've described, have you been experiencing persistent worry or anxiety? If so, what kinds of things do you tend to worry about?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, I worry about normal day-to-day things sometimes, but... no, I wouldn't say I have persistent anxiety. It's not like my mind is racing or anything like that. It's really just the tiredness and feeling kind of flat all the time. But as far as just constantly worrying about things, no, I haven't really experienced that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or your weight over these past two years?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My appetite has been pretty normal, I think. I haven't lost or gained any weight that I've noticed... my clothes all still fit the same as they always have. So, no, no real changes there.\n\nDoctor: Over these past two years since you moved into the house, has there been any stretch of time — even a week or two — where you felt like your normal self again, or has this low mood and fatigue been pretty much constant throughout that entire period?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I haven't really noticed any stretch where I felt completely back to normal. It's been pretty much constant... just this steady, flat feeling and the tiredness the whole time. If there was a good week or two in there, I honestly don't remember it. It just feels like it's been this way since we moved.\n\nDoctor: Besides the fatigue and sleep issues you've mentioned, have you noticed any physical symptoms — like muscle aches, headaches, or a general sense of heaviness or tension in your body — that have been going on along with the tiredness?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. It's honestly just... just the tiredness we talked about. So, no, no real physical pain or heaviness to go along with it.\n\nDoctor: Over these two years, have you ever had a period where you felt like you had more energy than usual, needed less sleep than normal, or felt like you were accomplishing things at a faster pace than your baseline?\n\nPatient: No, definitely not. It doesn't really seem to matter if I get a full night's sleep, I still wake up tired. And by the time I've handled work and tried to deal with the house clutter, I just have very little energy left for anything else. I'm just constantly exhausted.\n\nDoctor: Besides feeling exhausted, how has your mood been? Would you say you've been feeling down or depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I suppose I have been. It's... well, most days there's just this low, gray mood running in the background. Has it been going on for weeks, months, or longer?\n\nPatient: It's been longer than a few months. I'd say this sort of low baseline has been going on since... well, shortly after we moved into the older house. That was a little over two years ago now.\n\nDoctor: When you say you wake up tired even after a full night's sleep, can you tell me more about your sleep itself? Do you have trouble falling asleep, staying asleep through the night, or waking up too early in the morning?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't really have trouble falling asleep. I'm usually so tired by the end of the day that I drop off pretty quick. But staying asleep... I don't know, I guess I wake up a few times during the night. I don't really look at the clock, so I'm not sure if it's too early or what, but I just don't feel rested when the alarm finally goes off.\n\nDoctor: When you wake up during those nighttime awakenings, do you find it hard to fall back asleep, or do you drift back off pretty easily?\n\nPatient: I usually drift back off pretty easily. I might just adjust my pillow or shift around a bit, and then I'm back asleep. It's not like I'm lying there awake for hours or anything. I just... I don't know, I just wake up a few times, and then go right back to sleep.\n\nDoctor: Besides the low mood and fatigue you've described, have you been experiencing persistent worry or anxiety? If so, what kinds of things do you tend to worry about?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, I worry about normal day-to-day things sometimes, but... no, I wouldn't say I have persistent anxiety. It's not like my mind is racing or anything like that. It's really just the tiredness and feeling kind of flat all the time. But as far as just constantly worrying about things, no, I haven't really experienced that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or your weight over these past two years?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My appetite has been pretty normal, I think. I haven't lost or gained any weight that I've noticed... my clothes all still fit the same as they always have. So, no, no real changes there.\n\nDoctor: Over these past two years since you moved into the house, has there been any stretch of time — even a week or two — where you felt like your normal self again, or has this low mood and fatigue been pretty much constant throughout that entire period?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I haven't really noticed any stretch where I felt completely back to normal. It's been pretty much constant... just this steady, flat feeling and the tiredness the whole time. If there was a good week or two in there, I honestly don't remember it. It just feels like it's been this way since we moved.\n\nDoctor: Besides the fatigue and sleep issues you've mentioned, have you noticed any physical symptoms — like muscle aches, headaches, or a general sense of heaviness or tension in your body — that have been going on along with the tiredness?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I mean, I might get a normal headache every once in a while, but nothing out of the ordinary. My body doesn't really ache or feel tense or anything. It's honestly just... just the tiredness we talked about. So, no, no real physical pain or heaviness to go along with it.\n\nDoctor: Over these two years, have you ever had a period where you felt like you had more energy than usual, needed less sleep than normal, or felt like you were accomplishing things at a faster pace than your baseline?\n\nPatient: No, definitely not. I haven't had any bursts of energy or anything like that. I always need my sleep... probably more than I should, honestly. I haven't felt like I'm getting things done faster or better. It's just been that same low energy the whole time.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned the low mood and fatigue have been pretty constant for two years. I mean, I guess there might be days that aren't quite as bad as others, but... no, I wouldn't say the tiredness ever actually lifts. It doesn't really go away. It's just kind of always there in the background, so I can't really point to a specific day where I felt noticeably better or lighter.\n\nDoctor: During these past two years, has there been any stretch of time — say, two months or longer — where you felt completely free of this low mood and tiredness, even if it came back later?\n\nPatient: No, there hasn't been anything like that. I definitely haven't had a two-month break from it. Like I said, it's just been pretty constant for the last two years. My energy and mood were fine back then. I haven't really noticed any stretch where I felt completely back to normal. It's been pretty much constant... just this steady, flat feeling and the tiredness the whole time. Even if a day isn't quite as bad, the tiredness is still right there. It doesn't really get lighter. I think... I might just be dealing with it a little better on those days, but the actual tired feeling is always about the same.\n\nDoctor: Before you moved into the house two years ago, how was your mood and energy level? I don't think I've ever had a time where I felt unusually energetic or needed less sleep. My thoughts don't race, and I definitely haven't felt like I could take on more than usual. It's always just been this kind of... sluggishness, I guess. So no, nothing like what you're describing.\n\nDoctor: Looking back over these two years, has there ever been any stretch of two weeks in a row where you felt completely free of both the low mood and the tiredness — where you felt your normal self for that entire two-week period?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, there hasn't been any time where it just completely went away for two weeks straight. It's always just kind of there in the background, you know? I haven't had a break like that where I felt totally normal."
